[EVENT: TUR32843]
Date: 2023-12-05
Country: Turkey | Location: Van
Type: Strategic developments > Change to group/activity | Disorder: Strategic developments
Actor1: Government of Turkey (2016-) [State forces]
Fatalities: 0
Sources: Haberler; Kurdistan24; Sozcu [National-Regional]

DESCRIPTION:
Security measures: On 5 December 2023, Van Governor's office issued a ban on all forms of marches, protests, press conferences, sit-ins and other political gatherings in the province for 2 days in order to maintain public order and security. Similar measures have been imposed in the province (and several other southeastern provinces) for some years to maintain public order, presumably against political events that can be organized by Kurdish movements.

---

On 12 December 2023, residents of the 29 May Site (consisting of 134 apartments owned by the Turkey Diyanet Foundation) continued to demonstrate in front of the site in Uskudar, Istanbul, to protest the forced evacuation due to the implementation of the reserve area declaration under Law No. 6306. Despite the fact that the houses are not considered risky, the residents received evacuation notices within 29 days after the site was declared a reserve area on 10 October 2022. The residents have been holding a vigil in front of the site since 2 December 2023.

DESCRIPTION:
On 17 December 2023, Labor and Democracy Movement members staged a demonstration in Sur town, Diyarbakir, protesting against the defamation and insults targeting Sheikh Said who led a Kurdish nationalist rebellion against the new Republic of Turkey in 1925 and was executed along 46 others as a result. TTB, DBP, DEM and DTK representatives attended the event. The group claimed Sheikh Said is an important figure for Kurdish culture. Diyarbakir Municipality's recent attempt to name a street after Sheikh Saud sparked a new wave of controversy over him between Turkish nationalists who view him as a 'traitor' as per the official Turkish history thesis, and pro-Kurdish and Islamist groups considering him a 'martyr' of Kurdish or Islamic political movements respectively.
